Let's outline final algorithm:
- We'll work primarily with real sequence derived from binary search or continuous optimization.
- Fix n candidates: maybe powers of two 1024, 2048, 4096, and previous length 6962? For binary local search, n=4096 okay. But if best score target not reached at 4096, try 8192/16384? Larger n target maxD scales n, local search harder but maybe easier due averaging. We can allocate time.
- For each n, initialize several binary strings of weight k=floor(n/2) (or optimum weight p maybe 0.5). Use methods: random, chirp, Thue-Morse, van der Corput, low-discrepancy, previous height_sequence_1 thresholded? Then local search.

Binary local search details:
State: b (uint8 length n), D (int64 length 2n-1)=convolve(b,b). k constant. maxD via np.max(D) maybe recompute after each candidate. To speed, maintain D and current max? For simplicity and robustness, use vectorized flips and np.max.

Candidate generation for constant weight:
- Pick random one index i and random zero index j. Compute new D if swap i->0, j->1. Instead of applying/reverting, can compute net delta: remove i then add j. But we can just apply, compute new max, accept/revert.
- Applying removal: D[i:] -= 2*b[:n-i]; D[2i] += 1; b[i]=0.
- Adding: D[j:] += 2*b[:n-j]; D[2j] += 1; b[j]=1.
But after removal, b changed; addition uses updated b (with i=0, j=0). Net correct. To compute new max: `new_max = D.max()`. If new_max < best_max or accept probability, keep; else revert (apply inverse: add i then remove j?). Easier: copy b? Copy n each candidate costly. We can revert by applying inverse swaps: since j was 0->1, i 1->0. To revert: remove j (now1) then add i (now0) using same update functions. But must ensure formulas work. Yes.
- However D after apply may have small negative? Should not.
- We can precompute one indices and zero indices to pick random; update lists on swap.

But random swaps may be too slow to escape. We can do guided moves:
- Find a high lag s with value maxD. We want reduce it. For swap i->j, how affects a high s? If both i,j in pairs contributing to s, etc. Hard. Simulated annealing with random may work for n=1024 maybe. Need improve.

Alternative: Use local search on real weights with coordinate descent. Or use simulated annealing on bits with energy = ∑ exp(β D_s) to balance, not just max. Then minimize max at end. We can accept according to energy. But objective is max; energy with high beta good.

Metaheuristic ideas:
- Start from random; repeatedly choose a bit to flip that most reduces energy. For energy E=∑_s f(D_s) with convex f (e.g., D_s^2 or exp), flipping bit change can be computed as sum over affected lags: ΔE=∑_{p in affected} f(new)-f(old). Since affected count O(n), we can find best flip? Candidate bits n, each O(n) -> O(n^2) too much. But we can sample random flips and accept if energy decreases; optimal annealing.

Maybe use gradient-like multiplicative update directly on weights, no discrete bits.

Let's design a more effective binary local search:

A swap changes D at lags i+j and i? Let's derive net change for swapping i (1) out, j (0) in. For all other ones r, removing i decreases D[i+r] by2 (self etc), adding j increases D[j+r] by2. Plus self terms. This is like moving a mass from i to j: the set A changes. The convolution D is like the additive energy. We can view A as weights; moving one unit from i to j. Good: D_s = ∑_{r∈A} 1_{s-r∈A}. Moving i->j changes contributions involving r=i or j. Could approximate gradient for moving mass: If we allow fractional mass, objective changes. But discrete.

Simulated annealing with random swaps may slowly reduce max. We can accelerate by targeting moves from high-load positions to low-load positions. Define for a bit i, its "load vector" contribution to D. Moving one from i to j changes D by vector -2 b shifted by i plus self, plus +2 b shifted by j. To reduce current max, choose i and j such that high affected lags decrease and low lags increase. Hard.

Let's revisit LP idea; it might be more reliable than guessing.

We can implement LP-based SOTA for n maybe up to 2048. The LP has variables g≥0, constraints (f*g)_k ≤ M for all k, maximize sum g. For n=2048, constraints ~4095, vars 2048. HiGHS can solve maybe seconds. We can do e.g. 50-100 iterations. The sequence f can be continuous weights, not necessarily binary. This may converge to a good solution and perhaps below target. Let's detail implementation.

Objective scale invariant. Let normalization S0 = √(2n) or 1. Evaluator objective J=2n*M/S^2. If we normalize S=1, M_target = J/(2n). The LP with M=M_current (max f*f) and f current normalized sum=1. Solve max ∑g s.t. (f*g)_k≤M, g≥0. Since f itself feasible (f*f≤M, sum of g =1), optimum sum_g ≥1. Actually f is feasible, objective max sum g, so sum_g_opt ≥1. Rescale g to sum1: g = g_raw / sum_g_raw. Then mix f = (1-t) f + t g. Does this preserve/ reduce M? SOTA says with small t nonincreasing? Let's check: f is feasible with f*f≤M. g_raw satisfies f*g≤M. The mixture h=(1-t)f+tg has h*h = (1-t)^2 f*f +2t(1-t)f*g +t^2 g*g. We don't control g*g. Small t ensures h*h ≤ M + O(t^2) maybe; for small enough, not increase. Then normalize. Iterate. If sum_g_opt is large, g has more total mass; rescale may have different shape. This is like projection onto the polar of the cone? It may move to a sequence with lower max.

We can choose M lower than current to force improvement? If M=M_current, the LP maximum sum likely achieved by some extreme point maybe close to f? Need test. There may be many constraints binding; if f is far optimal, g could be different. The iteration reminiscent of "Dinkelbach" or "mutual averaging". We can implement with top constraints to speed.

Alternative: Use LP to solve for g that maximizes sum subject to (f*g)≤M and also perhaps g*g≤M? But g*g nonlinear. We can add constraints using previous g? Not.

We can use a simpler "dual ascent": The LP dual for max ∑g s.t. C g≤M, g≥0 is min M ∑λ_k s.t. ∑_k λ_k C_{k,j} ≥1. Here C_{k,j}=f_{k-j}. The optimal λ gives weights over lags. Then we can update f in direction? The active tight constraints indicate lags. We can use λ to form a subgradient of a Lagrangian: minimize max B_k? Maybe update f_{j} <- f_j * exp(-η∑λ_k f_{k-j}). This is multiplicative weights (blow-up) that reduces high convolution lags. This is similar to belief propagation / water-filling.

Specifically, define update g_j ∝ exp(-β ∑_k λ_k f_{k-j}), maybe. Since constraints f*g≤M, g small where f shifted by active lag large. Then mix with f. This can be done without LP if we compute λ by solving dual approximately or using active top lags equal weights.

Simpler heuristic: Maintain f normalized. At each step, compute B=f*f. Active top R lags (largest B). For each active lag k, compute y_j = ∑_{i: i+j=k?} f_i? Actually to reduce B_k, set g_j ∝ 1/f? Let's derive LP for equal weights on active lags: maximize sum g s.t. for k in A, (f*g)_k≤1 (ignoring M). The dual variables λ_k≥0. If we set a penalty P_j = λ_k f_{k-j}, then g_j small where P high. We can approximate by g_j = 1/(1+α P_j). This is an O(nR) computation if R small. Then normalize/mix. This is a fast heuristic.

Maybe the final program can perform a multi-stage random local search directly on a_i with FFT and accept improvements. Since we can run 1800s, a simple stochastic algorithm might suffice if the landscape not too bad. Let's design robust random local search with adaptive step size:
- Start with a variety of initial sequences: uniform, linear ramp, sine modulations, binary random, thresholded parent.
- For each, normalize sum1, nonnegative. Use objective via FFT.
- Perform a local search loop:
  * Propose a perturbation: choose a random interval/block and multiply by exp(N(0,σ)), or add/remove mass between two positions, or reflect/mirror part. Ensure nonnegative.
  * Optionally project to sum=1 (scale). Evaluate; if better, accept; else maybe accept with temperature (simulated annealing).
  * Adapt σ based on acceptance rate.
This is similar to previous program but more diverse and with temperature, multi-start. However previous block perturbation got only 2.0, likely because it didn't explore asymmetric shapes or was stuck at uniform. We can also use "mass transport" moves: pick two indices i,j and transfer mass δ from i to j (a_i-δ, a_j+δ) if a_i≥δ. This preserves sum and nonnegative. The objective change can be computed quickly using FFT? We can just recompute FFT for each candidate (n=1024, ~0.1ms? Python overhead maybe 1ms). 1800s could do 100k moves. Cancer. We can guide transfer from high-load positions to low-load positions: For current B, high lags; for mass at i, its contribution to B_k = a_i a_{k-i}. To reduce high lags, move mass from i to j that has lower partners. We can sample i among high contributing and j among low.

Simpler: use gradient/subgradient mass transfer. Compute active max lag k. Let g_i = a_{k-i}. We want reduce B_k: move mass from positions i with large g_i to positions j with small g_j. Choose i proportional to g_i (or a_i*g_i) and j with small a_j? Transfer δ. This is like doing descent on B_k while preserving sum. It may reduce active lag but increase others. We can perform several such transfers. This is a directed local search, not random.

Algorithm for continuous gradient transfer:
- Normalize a sum=1.
- Compute B via FFT, find top R active lags (maybe max).
- For each active lag k, define partner sum p_i = a_{k-i} (with zero out of range), and contribution derivative d_i=2p_i.
- We want move mass from high p_i to low p_i. But moving mass from i to j changes B_k by roughly -δ p_i + δ p_j (plus second order). So choose i with high p_i and j with low p_j. Also need avoid making a_i negative; transfer δ ≤ a_i*α. Apply to a.
- This reduces active B_k but may increase other lags. Use small step and maybe multiple active lags combined: define score s_i = weighted sum over active lags w_k a_{k-i}. Move mass from high s_i to low s_j. This is a subgradient descent on top lags.
- Normalize (sum preserved). Repeat.

This is like a "mean-field" balancing. We can vectorize s_i for top R: s = ∑_{k in top} w_k * rev_shift(a,k). We can transfer from high s to low s. This may converge to a balanced sequence. Let's test from uniform: top active center, s_i = a_{n-1-i}=1/n uniform, no movement. Need break symmetry by random initial or include local perturbations. Start from random asymmetric; top lags have different s, mass moves.

We can implement a "random asymmetric start" and run this mass-transfer for many iterations. This may find good local minima.

Alternatively, use "mirror flip" operations: The objective is invariant if sequence reversed? Actually B of reversed same set? Convolution max same. Asymmetry may be preserved? We can impose no symmetry.

Let's design final code phases:

A. Fast evaluator:
```
def fast_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a,float)
    n=len(a); s=a.sum()
    if s<=1e-12: return inf
    a=a/s
    N=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    b=np.real(np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N))[:2*n-1]
    # small negative clip
    return 2*n*b.max()
```
Since normalized sum1.

B. Initial generators:
- Parent threshold: `height_sequence_1` maybe. normalize, clip. It scored 2.0 uniform? maybe not. Use as candidate.
- Random nonnegative: exponential, gamma.
- Asymmetric shapes: linear ramp increasing/decreasing; sqrt ramp; power functions; two-sided with asymmetry: `x^p` and `(1-x)^q` mix.
- Sine/cosine modulations: a=1+ε sin(2πm i/n)+... maybe choose random m and coefficient. Ensure nonnegative by clipping, but clipping changes sum; use `a = 1 + ε * W` where W normalized to max abs1 and set ε ∈ [0,1] to keep nonnegative. Generate random Fourier W with zero mean.
- Binary local search outputs.

C. Continuous local optimizer:
Input a (sum1,n). Loop with time. We can use a temperature starting maybe 1e-4 and decay. Better to accept any improvement; use occasional restarts.
Moves:
1. Mass transfer:
   - Calculate B (if not cached) and top lags indices: choose top R (e.g., 5 or 10) by B value.
   - weights w maybe (B_k - median)^+ or normalized.
   - Compute `score = zeros(n)`. For each k with weight w, valid i range [max(0,k-n+1), min(n-1,k)], score[i] += w * a[k-i] (or just a? derivative). Could vectorize using slices: `score[i] += w * a[k-i]` for i slice; reversed a slice added to score.
   - Choose source indices distribution proportional to `score * a`? Source i with high score and positive a. Target indices with low score and a. We want transfer δ from source to target. If source score high, target low, decreases subgradient.
   - Select random source from top quantile of `score*a`, target from bottom quantile of `score` maybe. Transfer amount δ = a_source * step (e.g., 0.05..0.5). Ensure nonnegative. Update a.
   - Evaluate new score; accept if better or temperature.
2. Block scaling: random interval multiply by exp(normal(0,σ)). Normalize. Accept with probability.
3. Ripple: add small multiple of random Fourier basis.
4. Reflection/mirroring: replace part with reversed of another part? maybe.
Track best exact.

Will this escape uniform? Starting from uniform, score uniform -> no mass transfer. So initial random asymmetric essential. The parent is likely near uniform but random perturbations can break.

We can also explicitly create asymmetry by shifting a top-mass: e.g., take a_i = (1 + c * i/n) / normalize. Linear ramp may have objective maybe ~2? We can run optimizer.

D. Binary local search phase:
This may produce strong results. We need decide how much time. We can implement if n not too large (e.g., 512, 1024, 2048). It may find a binary sequence with score under target. Then convert to weights (1/k) and evaluate exact. We can also feed to continuous optimizer.

Binary local search implementation details more concretely:
- Choose lengths n = [256, 512, 1024, 2048] maybe. Time budget per length based on n: total maybe 600s. For each, k = max(1, int(round(n*0.5))). We can allow k between 0.35n and 0.65n? Objective =2n maxD/k^2. For a given D max, larger k reduces? If weights 1/k, objective ∝ 1/k^2 for same D; but D scales with k. The optimal density may not exactly 0.5. Random objective independent of k. We can try k = n//2, maybe also 0.4n,0.6n. But constant weight local search with different k? We'll fix k=n//2 to simplify. Could also nearest (n//2).
- Initial bitstrings:
  * random balanced: choose k indices.
  * Thue-Morse for n power of 2, weight maybe n//2. For n not power, take prefix.
  * m-sequence? Could generate LFSR for lengths 2^m-1 and maybe pad/truncate. But maybe skip.
  * Van der Corput / bit-reversal: b_i = 1 if floor(2*frac(i*α)<1) for α irrational, balanced.
  * Threshold of parent / Fourier.
- For each initial, compute D via np.convolve(b,b,'full') (int64). maxD. Convert to sequence: a = zeros(n); a[ones]=1/k; score = 2n*maxD/(k^2). Store best.
- Local search:
  Maintain b (np.uint8), ones_indices, zeros_indices. D int64. We can implement apply/remove functions with numpy slices.
  But we need efficient candidate evaluation. Suppose we do a random swap i (one) -> j (zero). We can apply, new_max=D.max(), accept if new_max < current_max or anneal. However D.max over 2n every iteration. n=2048 =>4095, fine. 100k iterations ~0.4s per loop? Actually D updates slices O(n) numpy but small; max O(n). Python overhead maybe 10 μs? 100k ~1s? Good.
  To improve, use a candidate set of potential swaps: Instead of random, choose i and j to likely reduce max:
    - Let high lags H = indices where D > current_max - threshold. We need reduce them. A swap i->j affects D at lags i+r and j+r. If i and j are paired to high lags, moving i to j may reduce. We can pick i from positions with high "incident load": incident_load_i = ∑_{s} D_s * something? Specifically, if b_i=1, it contributes to D_s for all s=i+j where b_j=1. The total contribution of bit i to vector D is 2 shift of ones by i plus self. To reduce high lags, choose i whose contribution overlaps high lags most. Compute incident_score_i = ∑_{j∈ones} high_weight[i+j]? That's O(k^2) if naive. But we can compute using convolution of high_weight with b: incident_i = (high_weight shifted by -i?) = ∑ high_weight[i+j] b_j. This is cross-correlation; can compute via FFT or direct? For sampled candidates, maybe update only for selected.
    - Simpler: For each high lag s, contributing pairs are i and s-i both ones. We can pick one bit i from such pairs as source, and pick a target j that is zero and whose addition to high lags less. Random among high pairs.
    - Implementation: get high_lags = np.where(D >= current_max - high_tol)[0]. For each high lag s, valid i range. We can choose random s, random i in range with b_i=1 and b_{s-i}=1 (if both). Source i. For target j, choose random zero j (or weighted to low load). Apply swap. This directly attacks high lag.
  This is more directed. We can do both random and directed.
- Temperature annealing: Accept swap if new_max < old_max, or with prob exp(-(new_max-old_max)/temp). new_max-old_max integer maybe. Start temp few, decay. Also maintain best sequence.
- To avoid long tether, if no improvement for many iterations, restart from current with random k? Or perturb by flipping several random swaps.
- Track best exact score by converting best b.

Will binary local search find target? Unknown. But combined with continuous maybe.

### Fast objective
```
def _pow2(n):
    m=1
    while m<n: m <<=1
    return m

def fast_score_normalized(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=1e-12: return np.inf
    a=a/s
    # clip tiny negatives? assume nonneg
    N=_pow2(2*n-1)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    b=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)
    b=b[:2*n-1].real
    # due roundoff, max may be slightly off. For screening okay.
    return float(2.0*n*max(0.0, b.max()))
```
If a has negative small, clip before: `a=np.maximum(a,0)`. But if we clip, sum changes; renormalize.
```
def normalize_nonneg(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float); a=np.maximum(a,0); s=a.sum(); if s<1e-12: return None; return a/s
```

Exact score via `evaluate_sequence(a.tolist())`. To avoid too many calls for FFT false positives? We'll call exact when fast score < best_exact - margin or periodically. Maintain best list.

def make_balanced_binary(n,k,rng):
    b=np.zeros(n,np.uint8)
    if k>=n: b.fill(1)
    else: idx=rng.choice(n,k,replace=False); b[idx]=1; return b
```

```

score = fast_score(a)

temp = 0.01

   if time out break

   top = np.argsort(B)[-R:] (descending)

   for k,w in zip(top, weights):
      # valid i
      lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)+1
      # partner indices: k - i (hi-1 down to lo)
      partner = np.roll? We can slice a reversed.
      seg_partner = a[k - (hi-1) : k - lo +1 : -1]? Need easier:

         General using roll? Simpler use for loop R<=5, vectorized with arange.
   # score_vec estimates derivative. We can add small random noise.

   # Normalize score_vec to percentile.
   sources = np.argwhere(score_vec > np.percentile(score_vec,80) & a > min_mass)
   targets = np.argwhere(score_vec < np.percentile(score_vec,20))
   if len sources and targets:
      i = rng.choice(sources) (rng.choice on array okay)
      j = rng.choice(targets)
      mass = min(a[i], total_mass*step_factor)
      # step_factor adaptive 0.01..0.5
      new = a.copy(); new[i]-=mass; new[j]+=mass

Let's detail binary local search with `runtime_s`.

```
def binary_search_one_start(b, k, n, budget_s, seed):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    D=np.convolve(b.astype(np.int64), b.astype(np.int64), 'full')
    cur_max=int(D.max())
    best_data = (b.copy(), D.copy(), cur_max)
    # lists
    ones=list(np.nonzero(b)[0]); zeros=list(np.nonzero(1-b)[0])
    # temperature
    temp=2.0
    last_improve=time.monotonic()
    it=0
    samples= max(2, min(8, 16384//n)) #?
    while time.monotonic() < t0:
       # maybe decay temp based on no improve
       if it % 100 ==0: temp = max(0.05, temp*0.999)
       # generate candidate swaps
       best_cand = None
       best_eval = cur_max
       # sample random swaps and maybe biased by high lags
       for _ in range(samples):
           # choose source one i
           idx_i = rng.integers(len(ones))
           i = ones[idx_i]
           # choose target zero j; maybe biased to positions not causing high? random
           idx_j = rng.integers(len(zeros))
           j = zeros[idx_j]
           if i==j: continue
           # apply swap
           apply_remove(b,D,i); apply_add(b,D,j)
           m=int(D.max())
           # keep candidate if m < best_eval or m==best_eval and len? We need store i,j and m
           if m < best_eval or (m == best_eval and best_cand is None and rng.random()<0.5):
               best_eval=m; best_cand=(i,j,idx_i,idx_j)
           # revert if not accepting yet? But we need know after samples if accept. We can revert each.
           apply_remove(b,D,j); apply_add(b,D,i)
       if best_cand is not None:
           i,j,idx_i,idx_j=best_cand
           # decide accept: if best_eval < cur_max or anneal
           delta = best_eval - cur_max
           accept = delta < 0
           if not accept and delta > 0:
               accept = rng.random() < math.exp(-delta/temp)
           if accept:
               # But candidate was reverted; apply it
               apply_remove(b,D,i); apply_add(b,D,j)
               # update lists
               # i removed from ones, added zeros; j from zeros to ones
               ones[idx_i]=ones[-1]; ones.pop()
               zeros.pop(idx_j)
               ones.append(j); zeros.append(i)
               cur_max=best_eval
               if cur_max < best_data[2]: best_data=(b.copy(),D.copy(),cur_max)
       it+=1
    return best_data
```
Problem: We revert candidate before updating lists, then apply. The stored idx_i/idx_j may have become invalid? We didn't modify lists during sampling, so valid. Good. But when applying after, we need b state original; lists still original. apply_remove uses b; okay.
However in sampling, we apply and revert using same i,j. If i/j in lists, okay. But apply_remove modifies b[D], then add j; revert remove j add i. After revert, D and b should exactly restore. Good.
Random source/target independent may be slow. We can add biased target: choose target j among zeros with lower current "incidence". But skip.

Annealing: If best_eval==cur_max and no improvement, accept random equal to shuffle. This helps.
Temperature should be in units of integer maxD. Starting temp maybe 3, decay. If n large, delta maybe 1-5. Set temp=1.0? We want move. Use `min(0.5, 2.0 * exp(-it/500))`? Since delta integer, exp(-1/1)=0.37, okay. Start 2.

Multiple starts:
```
def binary_search(n, k, budget_s, seed):
    starts = [balanced random, thue, van der corput, ramp]
    best = None
    for each start allocate remaining/budget/starts.
```
But each start local search may be expensive. We can do one strong start random, then greedy, then perturb.

Maybe continuous search better than binary random. We can run binary search for a few seconds, then continuous from best.

### Continuous local search detailed
Let's implement a guided continuous optimizer that may exploit binary init.

```
def continuous_search(a_init, budget_s, seed):
    a = normalize_nonneg(a_init).copy()
    n=a.size
    rng=...
    B, score = eval_fast(a) returns B and score normalized
    best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score
    # current best exact? in propose candidate manage
    step = 0.2 # mass fraction
    temp = 0.02
    R_top = 3
    while time < deadline:
       # if no improvements for many, perturb
       B, score = fast_conv(a)
       # choose top lags with some randomness
       B_sorted = np.sort(B)[::-1]
       thresh = B_sorted[min(R_top-1, len)-1]
       active = np.where(B >= thresh)[0]
       # choose subset up to 5 via sampling
       if active.size >5: active = rng.choice(active,5,replace=False)
       # weights proportional B
       w = B[active]; w/=w.sum()
       score_vec=np.zeros(n)
       for k in active:
           lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
           if lo<=hi:
              idx=np.arange(lo,hi+1)
              score_vec[lo:hi+1] += w_each * a[k-idx]
       # Add noise
       score_vec = score_vec + rng.normal(0, 1e-6, n)
       # Source high, target low
       # To avoid selecting empty, percentiles based on score_vec
       p_high = np.partition(score_vec, int(0.8*n))[-1]?
       sources = np.where((score_vec >= high_thresh) & (a > 1e-8))[0]
       targets = np.where(score_vec <= low_thresh)[0]
       if len(sources)==0 or len(targets): sources=np.argwhere(a>...) ; targets all.
       i = sources[rng.integers(len(sources))]
       j = targets[rng.integers(len(targets))]
       if i==j: continue
       # transfer amount: step * a[i], step adapt
       m = min(a[i], a[i]*step + 1e-9) # actually transfer <=a[i]
       # Maybe move a fraction frac of a[i]
       frac = min(1.0, step) # start 0.2
       m = a[i]*frac
       new=a.copy(); new[i]-=m; new[j]+=m
       new=normalize_nonneg(new) # sum same
       Bn, sn=fast_conv(new)
       delta=sn-score
       if sn < best_score: best_a=new; best_score=sn
       if sn < score or rng.random() < math.exp(-delta/temp):
           a=new
           score=sn
           step*=1.05? adapt
       else:
           step*=0.95
       # decay temp
       temp=max(1e-5,temp*0.999)
       # periodically check exact
```
But computing top active with `np.argsort(B)` O(n log n) each iter maybe heavy; we can just `np.sort` or use `np.argmax` for top R via `np.argpartition`. Use `top = np.argpartition(B, -R)[-R:]`.
For R=5.

def consider_candidate(arr, best, best_score):
    arr = normalize_nonneg(arr)
    if arr is None: return best,best_score
    seq=[float(x) for x in arr]
    sc=evaluate_sequence(seq)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
       return seq, float(sc)
    return best,best_score
```

Block scaling move:
```
def perturb_block(a,rng):
    n=a.size; width=int(rng.integers(max(2,n//50), max(4,n//3)))
    start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width+1)); stop=min(n,start+width)
    new=a.copy()
    new[start:stop] *= math.exp(float(rng.normal(0.0,0.3)))
    new=np.maximum(new,0); s=new.sum(); if s>0: new/=s
    return new
```

```
def _next_pow2(x):
    y=1
    while y<x: y <<=1
    return y
```

def lp_iteration(f, M, active_lags):
    n=f.size
    c=-np.ones(n)
    # Build A_ub rows for active lags: row k, variable j coefficient f_{k-j} if valid.
    rows=[]
    for k in active_lags:
        row=np.zeros(n)
        # j valid: max(0,k-n+1) ≤ j ≤ min(n-1,k)
        lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
        row[lo:hi+1] = f[k - (hi-lo?)? Need mapping: for j in lo..hi, coeff f[k-j]
        row[lo:hi+1] = f[lo:hi+1]? Want f[k-(lo:hi+1)].
        js=np.arange(lo,hi+1)
        row[js] = f[k - js]
        rows.append(row)
    A=np.vstack(rows)
    res=opt.linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=np.full(len(active_lags), M), bounds=(0,None), method='highs')
    if res.status==0: return res.x
    return None
```

#### Helper functions details

`normalize_nonneg(a)`:
```
def normalize_nonneg(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = a.sum(dtype=np.float64)
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return None
    a = a / s
    # cap 1000 not necessary since sum1, max≤1.
    return a
```
If a has negative from FFT? okay.

`fast_conv(a)`:
```
def fast_conv(a):
    a = np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    N=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    b=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)
    b=b[:2*n-1]
    # clip tiny negative
    if b.size:
       b_max=float(b.max())
       if b_max < 0: b_max=0.0
       return b, float(2.0*n*b_max)
    b_max=0.0
    return b, 0.0
```
For very small n, okay.

`fast_score(a)` just returns fast_conv score.

`consider_sequence(a,best_seq,best_score,deadline=None)`: exact evaluate. But exact evaluator may be slow; we call when `a` not None.
```
def consider_sequence(a, best_seq, best_score):
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    if a is None: return best_seq,best_score
    # quick exact? We can first fast score to avoid exact if fast > best_score + 0.1? But exact is authoritative; fast approximate.
    _, fs = fast_conv(a)
    if fs > best_score + 0.05:
        return best_seq,best_score
    seq=[float(x) for x in a]
    sc=evaluate_sequence(seq)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
        return seq, float(sc)
    return best_seq,best_score
```
But relying on fast to skip exact could miss if fast underestimates by >0.05; unlikely. For random initial many, maybe skip. For candidates near best, exact. We can remove skip and exact all initial? For local search many, use fast only. For top-level candidates maybe 100, exact direct n small okay. We can just exact all generated initial (maybe 200) with n<=512. For n=4096, direct exact 200*0.05s=10s okay. Limit.

```
a=normalize_nonneg; if None return; seq=list; sc=evaluate_sequence(seq); update
```

`gen_fourier(n, rng, R, amp)`:
```
w=np.zeros(n)
for r in range(1,R+1):
   ang=2*math.pi*r*np.arange(n)/n
   w += rng.normal()*np.sin(ang) + rng.normal()*np.cos(ang)
w -= w.mean()
mx=max(1e-12, abs(w).max())
w/=mx
a=1.0 + amp*w
a=np.maximum(a,0)
return normalize_nonneg(a)
```

`gen_gaussian_mixture(n, rng, m)`:

x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
f=zeros
for _ in range(m):
   c=float(rng.uniform(0,1)); sigma=float(rng.uniform(0.02,0.3)); amp=float(rng.uniform(0.1,1.0))
   f += amp*np.exp(-((x-c)**2)/(2*sigma*sigma))
return normalize_nonneg(f)
```
Can have endpoint issues but okay.
`gen_thue_bits(n,k)`:
```
b=np.zeros(n,np.uint8)
# choose parity 0 or 1 whichever closer to k
for p in [0,1]: assign bits where popcount(i)%2==p. Count. choose closer or adjust.
idx=np.nonzero(b)[0]; if count<k add random zeros; if >k remove random ones.
```
Python loop n up to4096 okay.
`gen_balanced_bits(n,k,rng,kind)`:
- random: `idx=rng.choice(n,k,replace=False); b[idx]=1`
- thue: above
- dx: `b = (np.floor(np.arange(n)*0.61803398875) % 2).astype(np.uint8)`; adjust to k.
- block: create runs length L with alternating 1, adjust.
 We'll implement `make_bits_target(n,k,b0,rng)` to adjust count.
```
def make_bits_target(n,k,b0,rng):
   b=b0.astype(np.uint8).copy()
   cnt=int(b.sum())
   if cnt<k:
      zeros = np.nonzero(b==0)[0]
      take = rng.choice(zeros, k-cnt, replace=False)
      b[take]=1
   elif cnt>k:
      ones=np.nonzero(b==1)[0]
      take=rng.choice(ones, cnt-k, replace=False)
      b[take]=0
   return b
```
If zeros empty? k<n.

#### Binary search
We'll write `binary_bits_search(n, k, budget_s, seed, max_starts=3)`.
Inside, for each start kind budget = remaining/starts.
Function `binary_optimize_bits(b,n,k,budget_s,seed)` returns best bits and maxD.
We need define `apply_add` and `apply_remove` top-level with n? Use `D` length = 2*n-1. In functions, n can be `len(b)`, but for speed pass n.
```
def apply_add(b,D,i,n):
    seg = b[:n]  # all b
    D[i:i+n] += 2 * seg
    D[2*i] += 1
    b[i] = 1
```
But if b uint8, `2*seg` uint16. D int64; okay but might upcast? Fine. To be safe `D[i:i+n] += seg.astype(np.int64); D[i:i+n] += seg.astype(np.int64)`.
`apply_remove`: similar subtract.
When applying remove, b[i] is 1. After subtract, set b[i]=0. For add, set after? In formula, addition updates D using old b (b_i=0) then sets 1. Removal uses old b (b_i=1) then sets 0. So:
```
def apply_add(...):
   if b[i]: return? avoid double.
   # old b_i=0
   D[i:i+n] += 2*b
   D[2*i] += 1
   b[i]=1
def apply_remove(...):
   if not b[i]: return
   D[i:i+n] -= 2*b
   D[2*i] += 1
   b[i]=0
```
Wait removal `D[i:i+n] -= 2*b` with b_i=1 subtract2 at self, then +1 net -1. Then set b=0. Good.
Addition with b_i=0 no self in vector, +1. Good.

But if apply_swap: remove i then add j. After remove, b_i=0; j still0. Good.
Revert: remove j, add i.

In sampling, we apply candidates and revert. Since `apply_add` uses current b; after revert state restored. Good.

`D` can be large int. `int(D.max())`.

`binary_optimize_bits` code with time. Need maintain lists. Choose `samples` based on n:
```
if n <=512: samples=8
elif n<=2048: samples=4
else: samples=2
```
Temperature: `temp=2.0`; each 500 iters `temp=max(0.1, temp*0.9)`.
Iteration count unlimited until budget.
To prevent infinite if lists empty, choose k between1,n-1.
When candidate `best_eval == cur_max`, accept equal with probability 0.2 to shuffle.
Track `record_best` if cur_max < best_max.
Return best bits copy, best_max.

#### Continuous search
`continuous_optimize(a_init, budget_s, seed)` returns best a.
It should use internal fast score, and maybe exact not. Propose will exact final. But to update global best, maybe pass `best_seq,best_score` and use consider when fast best improves. That's fine: after each phase, exact the phase best.

Inside continuous, maintain best_a by fast score. It doesn't call exact. Ensure final proposal exact.

Implementation details continuous:
```
def continuous_optimize(a_init, budget_s, seed):
   a=normalize_nonneg(a_init).copy()
   n=a.size
   if a is None: return None
   rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
   B, score = fast_conv(a)
   best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score
   step=0.2; temp=0.05; it=0
   deadline=time.monotonic()+budget_s
   no_improve=0
   while time.monotonic()<deadline:
      if no_improve > 5000:
          # perturb current
          a = perturb_block(a, rng)
          best? no
          step=min(0.8, step*1.5); no_improve=0
      B, score = fast_conv(a)
      if score < best_score - 1e-12:
          best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score; no_improve=0
      else: no_improve +=1
      # active lags
      Rtop = 3 if n<1000 else 5
      if B.size <= Rtop:
          active=np.nonzero(B>0)[0]
      else:
          active = np.argpartition(B, -Rtop)[-Rtop:]
      # maybe include random active with B > 0.8 max
      # weights
      wb=B[active].astype(float); wb[wib<=0]=1e-12; wb/=wb.sum()
      scv=np.zeros(n)
      for idx,k in enumerate(active):
          k=int(k); w=float(wb[idx])
          if k < n:
              scv[:k+1] += w * a[k::-1]
          else:
              start=k-n+1; length=2*n-1-k
              scv[start:n] += w * a[n-1 : n-1-length : -1]
      # noise if flat
      if scv.max()-scv.min() < 1e-12:
          scv = rng.normal(size=n)
      high = np.percentile(scv, 90)
      low = np.percentile(scv, 10)
      src = np.where((scv >= high) & (a > 1e-9))[0]
      tgt = np.where(scv <= low)[0]
      if src.size==0: src=np.where(a > a.max()*1e-3)[0]
      if tgt.size==0: tgt=np.arange(n)
      i=int(src[rng.integers(src.size)])
      j=int(tgt[rng.integers(tgt.size)])
      if i==j: continue
      frac = step
      m = a[i]*frac
      # try also maybe move to multiple targets? no
      new=a.copy(); new[i]-=m; new[j]+=m
      new=normalize_nonneg(new)
      if new is None: continue
      Bn, sn = fast_conv(new)
      if sn < best_score:
         best_a=new.copy(); best_score=sn
      delta=sn-score
      if delta < 0 or (delta <= 1 and rng.random() < math.exp(-delta/temp)):
         a=new; score=sn; step=min(0.9, step*1.03); no_improve=0
      else:
         step=max(0.02, step*0.97)
      # block perturbation occasionally
      if it % 100 == 0:
         bl = perturb_block(a, rng)
         Bbl, sbl=fast_conv(bl)
         if sbl < best_score: best_a=bl.copy(); best_score=sbl
         if sbl < score or rng.random()<math.exp(-(sbl-score)/temp):
             a=bl; score=sbl; no_improve=0
      if it % 500 ==0:
         temp=max(1e-4, temp*0.7)
      it +=1
   return best_a
```
`perturb_block` top-level.
`np.percentile` on float array.
Potential issue: The source selection from high score_vec and target low may move mass from index with high partner sum to low. If a[i] small, m small; okay.
If source is target same due percentiles? maybe.
`new=normalize_nonneg` after transfer sum same but if m > a[i]? no.
`math.exp(-delta/temp)` if delta negative okay (<1); if positive large underflows to0; use if delta/temp<700 else0.

This may be slow due FFT every iteration. For n=1024, budget 300s maybe 100k? FFT+percentile maybe 0.5ms ->50s for100k. Good. But Python overhead maybe 1ms ->300s for300k. Accept.

#### LP phase
We'll implement `lp_improve(a, budget_s, seed)` optional.
```
def lp_improve(a, budget_s, seed):
   try import scipy...
   except: return a
   a=normalize...
   n=a.size
   if n>1024: return a
   B, score=fast_conv(a)
   best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score
   deadline=time.monotonic()+budget_s
   it=0
   while time<deadline:
      M=max(B)
      # active top min(128, n)
      R = min(128, max(32, n//8))
      active = np.argpartition(B, -R)[-R:]
      # Build A rows
      rows=[]
      for k in active:
          row=np.zeros(n)
          lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
          if lo<=hi:
             js=np.arange(lo,hi+1)
             row[js]=a[k-js]
          rows.append(row)
      A=np.vstack(rows)
      c=-np.ones(n)
      res=opt.linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=np.full(A.shape[0], M), bounds=(0,None), method='highs')
      if res.status !=0:
          # decrease R? or break
          break
      g=res.x
      sg=g.sum()
      if sg <=1e-12: break
      g/=sg
      t=0.25
      mix=normalize_nonneg((1-t)*a + t*g)
      if mix is None: break
      Bm, sm=fast_conv(mix)
      if sm < best_score:
         a=mix.copy(); best_a=mix.copy(); best_score=sm; B=bm; score=sm
      it +=1
   return best_a
```
Need store `Bm`. In code, if not improve, maybe try smaller t or break after several. LP may be slow. Limits: if time budget >120 and n<=768? We can try n<=1024 with R<=64.
Potential issue: `b_ub=np.full(..., M)` M is max of all B, active constraints same. If M=0? no.
If LP returns g=0? maybe. Use bounds (0,None).
This LP can be called after continuous. But code length large; okay.

def propose_candidate(seed=42, **kwargs):
    deadline = time.monotonic() + 1770
    best_seq=None; best_score=np.inf
    # If height_sequence_1 exists, consider
    parent = globals().get('height_sequence_1')
    if parent is not None: try exact after normalize.
    # generate small n candidates
    for n in [64,128,256,512]:
       if time>deadline break
       rng=...
       for each generator produce maybe 5 candidates exact.
    # binary search phase
    for n in [512,1024,2048]:
       k=n//2
       bits, dmax = binary_bits_search(n,k,budget=... seed)
       seq = zeros; seq[bits]=1/k; consider exact.
       # continuous from binary
       a=seq; cont=continuous_optimize(a,budget=...); consider exact.
    # larger continuous from parent/ random
    for n in [1024,2048,4096]:
       a=gen random/gaussian; cont=continuous_optimize(a,budget=...); exact.
    # LP on best_seq if len<=1024 and time left
    if len(best_seq)<=1024 and time left >200:
       a=lp_improve(np.array(best_seq), min(300, timeleft), seed)
       exact
    # final consider parent if not
    if best_seq is None: best_seq=[1.0]*256? exact
    # ensure before deadline return
    return best_seq
```

### Let's further improve binary search with high-lag directed moves
Within `binary_optimize_bits`, after current_max, get `high_lags = np.where(D >= cur_max - 2)[0]`. For directed candidate selection, choose a random high lag s. We need find a one pair contributing. Instead of scanning all i each time, we can precompute for each high lag a list of one pair candidates? Too heavy.
Simpler: choose i from ones that are "central" (near n/2) because they contribute to high center lags. Target j from zeros far? This may reduce center but not all. We can mix: 30% directed: i = random one with high index? Actually all ones contribute to center with partner n-1-i. If center high, pairs with both ones. Choosing a random one i doesn't guarantee partner one. We can sample i and if b[n-1-i]==1, source i; else random. This attacks center. For other high lags, as local search reduces center, other lags become high. We can generalize: Choose a random lag s from top `H`, then choose i in valid range such that b[i]=1 and b[s-i]=1. To find one, we can sample up to T random i in valid range until find both. This costs T. Range length up to n. For a dense set, probability both ones ~p^2=0.25, so T~4. Good. Then source i. Target j: choose a zero j; we can bias to positions where b_{s-j?} maybe not one for same high lag? To reduce s after swap i->j, new D_s loses pair (i,s-i) and gains (j,s-j) if both ones. We want j such that b[s-j]=0 to not restore. We can sample zeros j until b[s-j]==0. Probability ~0.5. Good. This directed swap directly reduces a high lag.
Implement in candidate sampling:
- Maintain a list of high_lags refreshed every 50 iterations: `high_lags = np.where(D >= max(D) - 3)[0]` (or top 100 by value: `np.argpartition(D,-100)`).
- For each candidate, with prob 0.5 directed:
   * choose s from high_lags randomly
   * valid i range [max(0,s-n+1), min(n-1,s)]
   * try up to 8 random i in range until b[i] and b[s-i] both 1. If found source i.
   * try up to 8 random j in range? j must be zero and to not immediately restore at s: if 0<=s-j<n and b[s-j]==1 avoid. But j can be outside valid range for s (then D_s no new contribution), even better. We can choose j random zero overall, but not too costly. Simpler choose random zero j; if s-j in [0,n-1] and b[s-j]==1, maybe reject and try. It reduces high lag on average.
- If directed fails, random swap.
This should help.

Need high_lags list as Python list. Refresh:
```
idx = np.argpartition(D, -max(20, min(100, D.size//10)))[-R:]
high_lags = list(idx)
```
This costs O(n) every 50; fine.

In candidate loop, choose directed if high_lags not empty.
Selecting random i in range: `lo=int(max(0,s-n+1)); hi=int(min(n-1,s))`; `i=int(rng.integers(lo, hi+1))`.
For j random zero: `idx_j=rng.integers(len(zeros)); j=zeros[idx_j]`. Optionally try a few:
```
for _ in range(4):
  j=zeros[rng.integers(len(zeros))]
  sj=s-j
  if sj<0 or sj>=n or not b[sj]: break
```
If none, accept last.

### Let's draft final code carefully now.

Code details for `binary_optimize_bits`:
```
def binary_optimize_bits(b, budget_s, seed):
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.uint8).copy()
    n = b.size
    k = int(b.sum())
    if k <= 0 or k >= n: return b.copy()
    D = np.convolve(b.astype(np.int64), b.astype(np.int64), mode='full')
    cur_max = int(D.max())
    best_b = b.copy(); best_max = cur_max
    ones = list(np.nonzero(b)[0])
    zeros = list(np.nonzero(1-b)[0])
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, budget_s)
    temp = 1.5
    it = 0
    high_lags = list(np.argpartition(D, -min(100, max(10, D.size//10)))[::-1])? argpartition returns unsorted; okay.
    def? no nested. We'll implement loops.
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       if it % 50 == 0:
          R = min(100, max(10, D.size//10))
          high_lags = list(np.argpartition(D, -R)[-R:])
          temp = max(0.05, temp*0.98)
       samples = 2 if n > 2048 else (3 if n>512 else 5)
       best_eval = cur_max
       best_move = None
       for _ in range(samples):
          # choose move
          directed = (len(high_lags)>0 and rng.random() < 0.5)
          i=j=-1
          if directed:
             s = int(high_lags[rng.integers(len(high_lags))])
             lo = max(0, s-n+1); hi=min(n-1,s)
             found=False
             for _try in range(8):
                ii=int(rng.integers(lo, hi+1))
                jj=s-ii
                if 0 <= jj < n and b[ii] and b[jj] and ii != jj:
                   i=ii; found=True; break
             if found:
                # choose j
                for _try in range(6):
                   idxj=int(rng.integers(len(zeros)))
                   jj=int(zeros[idxj])
                   sj=s-jj
                   if sj<0 or sj>=n or not b[sj]:
                      j=jj; break
                if j is None: j=int(zeros[rng.integers(len(zeros))])
             else:
                # random fallback
                i=int(ones[rng.integers(len(ones))])
                j=int(zeros[rng.integers(len(zeros))])
          else:
             i=int(ones[rng.integers(len(ones))])
             j=int(zeros[rng.integers(len(zeros))])
          if i==j: continue
          # apply
          apply_remove(b,D,i,n)
          apply_add(b,D,j,n)
          m=int(D.max())
          if m < best_eval or (m == best_eval and best_move is None):
              best_eval=m; best_move=(i,j)
          # revert
          apply_remove(b,D,j,n)
          apply_add(b,D,i,n)
       if best_move is not None:
          delta = best_eval - cur_max
          accept = delta < 0
          if not accept:
             if delta == 0:
                accept = rng.random() < 0.2
             else:
                # avoid underflow
                if delta > 50: accept=False
                else: accept = rng.random() < math.exp(-float(delta)/temp)
          if accept:
             i,j=best_move
             # update lists: need find positions? We can use lists and remove by value with swap. Since we didn't store indices, use values.
             # Remove i from ones, j from zeros efficiently by linear search? n small; but doing linear each accept O(n) okay? To optimize, maintain dictionaries pos? Simpler: use lists and `remove` O(n) might be okay if accepts not many (thousands *2000=2M). okay.
             if i in ones: ones.remove(i)
             if j in zeros: zeros.remove(j)
             ones.append(j); zeros.append(i)
             apply_remove(b,D,i,n)
             apply_add(b,D,j,n)
             cur_max=best_eval
             if cur_max < best_max:
                 best_b=b.copy(); best_max=cur_max
       it +=1
    return (best_b, int(best_max))
```
Using `list.remove` O(n) but okay. To avoid if i not in ones (should be), but after apply/revert state same.
Potential bug: `j` may be uninitialized if directed found false and random path? In Python, set default i=j=-1; if directed found true, set j maybe; if directed found false, set random. If not directed, random. If i==j continue; but `j` could remain -1 if directed found true but zero list empty? k<n so zeros not empty. Good.
When high_lags list from argpartition unsorted; choosing random fine.
`apply_remove` on j during sampling: b[j]=0, apply_add works. During revert: after first candidate applied and reverted, state restored. Good.

But in candidate sampling, if we apply remove i then add j, then `D.max()`. If i/j chosen unequally, D values maybe. Good.

One inefficiency: The candidate is reverted before possible update; if many candidates, repeated. Okay.

State of `ones`/`zeros` after accepted move: We do `ones.remove(i); zeros.remove(j); ones.append(j); zeros.append(i)`. `remove` loop each accept. Fine.

`np.argpartition(D, -R)` with D int64; if R==D.size? Use R < D.size; D.size=2n-1, R min(100,D.size//10), for n small maybe R=10. If D.size//10=0 for n=1; n≥64. Good.

Function `apply_remove/add` must not use `n` bigger than D size? D length 2n-1, slice i:i+n length n, max i=n-1 -> end 2n-1 <= D length. Good.

### Continuous `perturb_block`
```
def perturb_block(a,rng):
   n=a.size
   if n<3: return a.copy()
   wlo=max(2,n//100); whi=max(wlo+1,n//2)
   width=int(rng.integers(wlo, whi+1))
   start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width+1))
   stop=start+width
   new=a.copy()
   new[start:stop] *= math.exp(float(rng.normal(0.0, 0.25)))
   new=np.maximum(new,0.0)
   return normalize_nonneg(new)
```
Ensure whi > wlo; if n small.

```python
import numpy as np
import time
import math

try:
    import scipy.optimize as _scipy_opt
    HAVE_SCIPY_OPT = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_SCIPY_OPT = False


def _next_pow2(x):
    y = 1
    while y < x:
        y <<= 1
    return y


def normalize_nonneg(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return None
    a = a / s
    return a


def fast_conv(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(1, dtype=np.float64), 0.0
    N = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    b = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)
    b = b[:2 * n - 1].real
    if b.size == 0:
        return b, 0.0
    mx = float(b.max())
    if mx < 0.0:
        mx = 0.0
    return b, float(2.0 * n * mx)


def fast_score(a):
    _, s = fast_conv(a)
    return s


def try_exact(a, best_seq, best_score):
    a = normalize_nonng(a) if False else normalize_nonneg(a)
    if a is None:
        return best_seq, best_score
    _, fs = fast_conv(a)
    if best_score < np.inf and fs > best_score + 0.05:
        return best_seq, best_score
    seq = [float(x) for x in a]
    sc = evaluate_sequence(seq)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
        return seq, float(sc)
    return best_seq, best_score


def gen_fourier(n, rng, R, amp):
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for r in range(1, R + 1):
        ang = 2.0 * math.pi * r * x / float(n)
        w += rng.normal() * np.sin(ang)
        w += rng.normal() * np.cos(ang)
    w -= float(np.mean(w))
    mx = float(np.max(np.abs(w)))
    if mx > 0.0:
        w /= mx
    a = 1.0 + amp * w
    return normalize_nonneg(a)


def gen_gaussian_mix(n, rng, m):
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    f = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for _ in range(m):
        c = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0))
        sigma = float(rng.uniform(0.02, 0.35))
        amp = float(rng.uniform(0.1, 1.0))
        f += amp * np.exp(-((x - c) * (x - c)) / (2.0 * sigma * sigma))
    return normalize_nonneg(f)


def initial_candidates(n, rng):
    yield normalize_nonneg(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    for p in (0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0):
        yield normalize_nonneg(np.power(x, p) + 1e-12)
        yield normalize_nonneg(np.power(1.0 - x, p) + 1e-12)
        yield normalize_nonneg(np.power(x, p) + 0.5 * np.power(1.0 - x, p) + 1e-12)

    for R, amp in ((2, 0.45), (3, 0.6), (4, 0.75), (6, 0.9)):
        yield gen_fourier(n, rng, R, amp)

    for _ in range(4):
        yield gen_gaussian_mix(n, rng, 2)
        yield gen_gaussian_mix(n, rng, 3)


def make_bits_target(n, k, b0, rng):
    b = np.asarray(b0, dtype=np.uint8).copy()
    b = np.mod(b, 2).astype(np.uint8)
    if b.size != n:
        b = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.uint8)
    cnt = int(b.sum())
    if k <= 0:
        return b
    if k >= n:
        b.fill(1)
        return b
    if cnt < k:
        zeros = np.nonzero(b == 0)[0]
        need = k - cnt
        if zeros.size > 0 and need > 0:
            take = rng.choice(zeros, size=min(need, zeros.size), replace=False)
            b[take] = 1
    elif cnt > k:
        ones = np.nonzero(b == 1)[0]
        need = cnt - k
        if ones.size > 0 and need > 0:
            take = rng.choice(ones, size=min(need, ones.size), replace=False)
            b[take] = 0
    return b


def gen_balanced_bits(n, k, rng, kind):
    if kind == 0:
        b = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.uint8)
        idx = rng.choice(n, size=k, replace=False)
        b[idx] = 1
        return b
    if kind == 1:
        b = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.uint8)
        for i in range(n):
            if int(i).bit_count() % 2 == 0:
                b[i] = 1
        return make_bits_target(n, k, b, rng)
    if kind == 2:
        vals = np.floor(np.arange(n) * 0.61803398875)
        b = (vals % 2).astype(np.uint8)
        return make_bits_target(n, k, b, rng)
    if kind == 3:
        L = max(3, n // 8)
        b = ((np.arange(n) // L) % 2).astype(np.uint8)
        return make_bits_target(n, k, b, rng)
    return make_bits_target(n, k, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.uint8), rng)


def apply_add(b, D, i, n):
    # old b[i] must be 0
    if b[i]:
        return
    D[i:i + n] += b.astype(np.int64)
    D[i:i + n] += b.astype(np.int64)
    D[2 * i] += 1
    b[i] = 1


def apply_remove(b, D, i, n):
    # old b[i] must be 1
    if not b[i]:
        return
    D[i:i + n] -= b.astype(np.int64)
    D[i:i + n] -= b.astype(np.int64)
    D[2 * i] += 1
    b[i] = 0


def binary_optimize_bits(b_init, budget_s, seed):
    b = np.asarray(b_init, dtype=np.uint8).copy()
    n = b.size
    k = int(b.sum())
    if k <= 0 or k >= n:
        return b.copy(), int(k)
    D = np.convolve(b.astype(np.int64), b.astype(np.int64), mode='full')
    cur_max = int(D.max())
    best_b = b.copy()
    best_max = cur_max

    ones = list(np.nonzero(b)[0])
    zeros = list(np.nonzero(1 - b)[0])
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, budget_s)

    temp = 1.8
    it = 0

    def high_lags_update():
        R = min(120, max(12, D.size // 8))
        if R <= 0:
            return list(range(max(0, D.size - 1), D.size))
        if R >= D.size:
            return list(range(D.size))
        idx = np.argpartition(D, -R)[-R:]
        return list(int(z) for z in idx)

    high_lags = high_lags_update()

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if it % 60 == 0:
            high_lags = high_lags_update()
            temp = max(0.05, temp * 0.985)

        samples = 2
        if n <= 512:
            samples = 5
        elif n <= 2048:
            samples = 3

        best_eval = cur_max
        best_i = -1
        best_j = -1

        for _ in range(samples):
            i = -1
            j = -1
            directed_ok = len(high_lags) > 0 and rng.random() < 0.55

            if directed_ok and len(zeros) > 0 and len(ones) > 0:
                s = int(high_lags[int(rng.integers(len(high_lags)))])
                lo = max(0, s - n + 1)
                hi = min(n - 1, s)
                for _try in range(10):
                    ii = int(rng.integers(lo, hi + 1))
                    jj = s - ii
                    if 0 <= jj < n and b[ii] and b[jj] and ii != jj:
                        i = ii
                        break
                if i != -1:
                    for _try in range(8):
                        jj = int(zeros[int(rng.integers(len(zeros)))])
                        sj = s - jj
                        if sj < 0 or sj >= n or not b[sj]:
                            j = jj
                            break
                    if j == -1:
                        j = int(zeros[int(rng.integers(len(zeros)))])
                else:
                    i = int(ones[int(rng.integers(len(ones)))])
                    j = int(zeros[int(rng.integers(len(zeros)))])
            else:
                if len(ones) == 0 or len(zeros) == 0:
                    break
                i = int(ones[int(rng.integers(len(ones)))])
                j = int(zeros[int(rng.integers(len(zeros)))])

            if i == -1 or j == -1 or i == j:
                continue

            apply_remove(b, D, i, n)
            apply_add(b, D, j, n)
            m = int(D.max())
            if m < best_eval or (m == best_eval and best_i == -1):
                best_eval = m
                best_i = i
                best_j = j
            apply_remove(b, D, j, n)
            apply_add(b, D, i, n)

        if best_i != -1 and best_j != -1:
            delta = best_eval - cur_max
            accept = delta < 0
            if not accept:
                if delta == 0:
                    accept = rng.random() < 0.18
                elif delta <= 60:
                    accept = rng.random() < math.exp(-float(delta) / temp)
                else:
                    accept = False
            if accept:
                iold = best_i
                jnew = best_j
                # update lists
                try:
                    ones.remove(iold)
                except Exception:
                    pass
                try:
                    zeros.remove(jnew)
                except Exception:
                    pass
                if len(ones) < n:
                    ones.append(jnew)
                if len(zeros) < n:
                    zeros.append(iold)

                apply_remove(b, D, iold, n)
                apply_add(b, D, jnew, n)
                cur_max = best_eval
                if cur_max < best_max:
                    best_b = b.copy()
                    best_max = cur_max
        it += 1

    return best_b, int(best_max)


def binary_bits_search(n, k, budget_s, seed):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, budget_s)
    starts = (0, 1, 2, 3)
    active = []
    rem = float(budget_s)
    if rem <= 0.0:
        return None, 0
    for sk in starts:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 2.0:
            break
        if sk == 0 and n % 2 == 0 and n <= 8192:
            pass
        sub = rem / float(len(starts) - len(active))
        if sub < 5.0:
            sub = rem
        active.append(sk)
        b = gen_balanced_bits(n, k, rng, sk)
        bits, dmax = binary_optimize_bits(b, sub, seed + 1000 * (sk + 1))
        if bits is not None:
            return bits, dmax
    return None, 0


def perturb_block(a, rng):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float76).copy() if hasattr(np, 'float76') else a.astype(np.float64).copy()
    n = a.size
    if n < 5:
        return a.copy()
    wlo = max(4, n // 120)
    whi = max(wlo + 1, n // 2)
    width = int(rng.integers(wlo, whi + 1))
    start = int(rng.integers(0, n - width + 1))
    stop = start + width
    b = a.copy()
    b[start:stop] *= math.exp(float(rng.normal(0.0, 0.28)))
    b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
    return normalize_nonneg(b)


def continuous_optimize(a_init, budget_s, seed):
    a = normalize_nonneg(a_init)
    if a is None:
        return None
    n = a.size
    if n < 5:
        return a
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    B, score = fast_conv(a)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = score
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, budget_s)
    step = 0.22
    temp = 0.06
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        B, score = fast_conv(a)
        if score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_a = a.copy()
            best_score = score
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1

        if no_improve > 2500:
            a = perturb_block(a, rng)
            B, score = fast_conv(a)
            if score < best_score:
                best_a = a.copy()
                best_score = score
            no_improve = 0
            step = min(0.85, step * 1.3)

        Rtop = 3 if n < 1000 else 5
        if B.size <= Rtop:
            active = np.argsort(B)[::-1]
        else:
            active = np.argpartition(B, -Rtop)[-Rtop:]

        if active.size == 0:
            break
        wb = B[active].astype(np.float64)
        wb = np.maximum(wb, 1e-18)
        wb /= float(np.sum(wb))

        scv = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for idx in range(active.size):
            k = int(active[idx])
            w = float(wb[idx])
            if k < n:
                scv[:k + 1] += w * a[k::-1]
            else:
                start = k - n + 1
                length = 2 * n - 1 - k
                if length > 0:
                    scv[start:n] += w * a[n - 1:n - 1 - length:-1]

        if float(scv.max() - scv.min()) < 1e-13:
            scv = rng.normal(size=n) + 1e-3

        high_thresh = float(np.percentile(scv, 90))
        low_thresh = float(np.percentile(scv, 10))
        src = np.where((scv >= high_thresh) & (a > 1e-10))[0]
        tgt = np.where(scv <= low_thresh)[0]
        if src.size == 0:
            src = np.where(a > 0.5 * a.max())[0]
        if tgt.size == 0:
            tgt = np.arange(n)
        if src.size == 0 or tgt.size == 0:
            continue

        i = int(src[int(rng.integers(src.size))])
        j = int(tgt[int(rng.integers(tgt.size))])
        if i == j:
            continue

        m = float(a[i]) * step
        new = a.copy()
        new[i] -= m
        new[j] += m
        new = normalize_nonneg(new)
        if new is None:
            continue

        Bn, sn = fast_conv(new)
        if sn < best_score:
            best_a = new.copy()
            best_score = sn

        delta = sn - score
        if delta <= 0.0:
            a = new
            score = sn
            step = min(0.9, step * 1.04)
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            if delta <= 80.0:
                p = math.exp(-float(delta) / temp)
                if rng.random() < p:
                    a = new
                    score = sn
                    no_improve = 0
            step = max(0.03, step * 0.97)

        if it % 80 == 0:
            bl = perturb_block(a, rng)
            if bl is not None:
                Bbl, sbl = fast_conv(bl)
                if sbl < best_score:
                    best_a = bl.copy()
                    best_score = sbl
                delta_bl = sbl - score
                if delta_bl <= 0.0 or (delta_bl <= 100.0 and rng.random() < math.exp(-float(delta_bl) / temp)):
                    a = bl
                    score = sbl
                    no_improve = 0

        if it % 400 == 0:
            temp = max(1e-5, temp * 0.75)
        it += 1

    return best_a


def lp_improve(a_init, budget_s, seed):
    if not HAVE_SCIPY_OPT:
        return None
    a = normalize_nonneg(a_init)
    if a is None:
        return None
    n = a.size
    if n > 1024:
        return None
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    B, score = fast_conv(a)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = score
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, budget_s)
    stall = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if stall > 8:
            break
        maxB = float(B.max())
        if maxB <= 1e-14:
            break
        R = min(96, max(24, n // 6))
        if B.size <= R:
            active = np.argsort(B)[::-1]
        else:
            active = np.argpartition(B, -R)[-R:]
        rows = []
        for k in active:
            row = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            lo = max(0, int(k) - n + 1)
            hi = min(n - 1, int(k))
            if lo <= hi:
                js = np.arange(lo, hi + 1)
                row[js] = a[int(k) - js]
            rows.append(row)
        try:
            A = np.vstack(rows)
            res = _scipy_opt.linprog(
                -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64),
                A_ub=A,
                b_ub=np.full(A.shape[0], maxB, dtype=np.float64),
                bounds=(0.0, None),
                method='highs'
            )
        except Exception:
            break
        if res is None or getattr(res, 'status', 1) != 0:
            break
        g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
        g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
        sg = float(g.sum())
        if sg <= 1e-12:
            break
        g /= sg
        t = 0.22
        mix = normalize_nonneg((1.0 - t) * a + t * g)
        if mix is None:
            break
        Bm, sm = fast_conv(mix)
        if sm < best_score - 1e-12:
            a = mix.copy()
            B = Bm.copy()
            score = sm
            best_a = mix.copy()
            best_score = sm
            stall = 0
        else:
            stall += 1
    return best_a


def binary_to_best(bits, dmax, best_seq, best_score):
    if bits is None:
        return best_seq, best_score
    n = len(bits)
    k = int(bits.sum())
    if k <= 0:
        return best_seq, best_score
    score = 2.0 * float(n) * float(dmax) / (float(k) * float(k))
    if best_score < np.inf and score > best_score + 0.05:
        return best_seq, best_score
    a = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a[bits] = 1.0 / float(k)
    return try_exact(a, best_seq, best_score)


def protected_parent():
    p = globals().get('height_sequence_1', None)
    if p is None:
        return None
    try:
        return np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        return None


def propose_candidate(seed=42, **kwargs):
    seed = int(getattr(np, 'hash', lambda x: 0)(seed)) if False else int(seed)
    total_budget = 1770.0
    deadline = time.monotonic() + total_budget

    # Valid fallback.
    best_seq = [1.0] * 256
    try:
        best_score = float(evaluate_sequence(best_seq))
    except Exception:
        best_score = np.inf

    rng_global = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    # Parent, if available.
    parent = protected_parent()
    if parent is not None:
        pseq = parent
        try:
            best_seq, best_score = try_exact(pseq, best_seq, best_score)
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Short initial scans.
    for n in (96, 192, 384):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 60.0:
            break
        rng = np.random.default_rng(seed + 101 * n)
        count = 0
        for cand in initial_candidates(n, rng):
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 30.0:
                break
            if cand is None:
                continue
            best_seq, best_score = try_exact(cand, best_seq, best_score)
            count += 1
            if count > 36:
                break

    # Binary phases.
    for n, budget in ((384, 70.0), (768, 140.0), (1536, 200.0)):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 240.0:
            break
        k = max(1, n // 2)
        bits, dmax = binary_bits_search(n, k, budget, seed + 3000 * n)
        best_seq, best_score = binary_to_best(bits, dmax, best_seq, best_score)

        # Polish the binary candidate with a continuous move.
        if bits is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 120.0:
            a = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            kk = int(bits.sum())
            if kk > 0:
                a[bits] = 1.0 / float(kk)
                nt = min(60.0, max(10.0, 0.18 * budget))
                cont = continuous_optimize(a, nt, seed + 7000 * n)
                if cont is not None:
                    best_seq, best_score = try_exact(cont, best_seq, best_score)

    # Continuous refinement from a few good shapes.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 180.0:
        a = None
        if parent is not None and parent.size > 64 and parent.size <= 8192:
            a = parent.astype(np.float64)
        else:
            rng = np.random.default_rng(seed + 9191)
            a = gen_gaussian_mix(1024, rng, 3)
        cont = continuous_optimize(a, min(260.0, max(30.0, total_budget - (time.monotonic() - deadline))), seed + 13000)
        if cont is not None:
            best_seq, best_score = try_exact(cont, best_seq, best_score)

    # A smaller continuous scan if time remains.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 80.0:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(seed + 424242)
        for n, m in ((512, 2), (768, 3)):
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 40.0:
                break
            cand = gen_gaussian_mix(n, rng, m)
            if cand is None:
                continue
            cont = continuous_optimize(cand, min(45.0, max(5.0, 45.0)), seed + 60000 + n)
            if cont is not None:
                best_seq, best_score = try_exact(cont, best_seq, best_score)

    # LP polish, if available and likely to finish.
    if HAVE_SCIPY_OPT and best_seq is not None and len(best_seq) <= 1024 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 60.0:
        a = normalize_nonneg(np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64))
        lp = lp_improve(a, min(80.0, max(5.0, float(deadline - time.monotonic())) - 10.0), seed + 88000)
        if lp is not None:
            best_seq, best_score = try_exact(lp, best_seq, best_score)

    # One last exact check of candidate pool top? Just return best found.
    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = [1.0] * 256
        try:
            best_score = float(evaluate_sequence(best_seq))
        except Exception:
            best_score = 2.0

    return [float(x) for x in best_seq]
```
